I'm kind of scared I can't stop doing it now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well... it's been going on for a while. Um, for at least the past three months, I guess. There's hardly been a week where I didn't... you know, eat too much late at night and then... have to go get rid of it. Sometimes it's a few times a week. Especially lately, since I've been stuck at home more with a bad back. It just... happens.\n\nDoctor: When you say you 'eat too much', do you feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean... at night, when everything's quiet, I'll just stand in the kitchen. I'll eat whatever's there. Leftovers, cereal, chips... ice cream. I just keep going. Way past what a normal meal would be. By the time I stop, half the pantry looks picked over. So... yeah, it's a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean... if the scale goes up even a pound, I... I kind of panic. Being laid up with my back, I just feel like I'm turning soft. So... I just start figuring out how to undo it. Before I can even really think straight.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from what it really is?\n\nPatient: I don't know about different... I just know what I see. When I put on my work pants and they feel even a little tight, or... or I see my stomach in the mirror, it's just... I don't know. It feels like I'm failing. Like I'm losing who I am. I used to be strong, you know? I could pull transmissions, keep up with the guys at the shop... and now I just see this person who can't even fit in her clothes right. It just... it takes over how I feel about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you knew for certain that your weight and body shape were perfectly healthy?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean, I guess it's hard to imagine not worrying about it. It just... happens.\n\nDoctor: When you say you 'eat too much', do you feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean... at night, when everything's quiet, I'll just stand in the kitchen. I'll eat whatever's there. Leftovers, cereal, chips... ice cream. I just keep going. Way past what a normal meal would be. By the time I stop, half the pantry looks picked over. So... yeah, it's a lot.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean... if the scale goes up even a pound, I... I kind of panic. Being laid up with my back, I just feel like I'm turning soft. So... I just start figuring out how to undo it. Before I can even really think straight.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from what it really is?\n\nPatient: I don't know about different... I just know what I see. When I put on my work pants and they feel even a little tight, or... or I see my stomach in the mirror, it's just... I don't know. It feels like I'm failing. Like I'm losing who I am. I used to be strong, you know? I could pull transmissions, keep up with the guys at the shop... and now I just see this person who can't even fit in her clothes right. It just... it takes over how I feel about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you knew for certain that your weight and body shape were perfectly healthy?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean, I guess it's hard to imagine not worrying about it. It just... it doesn't really matter what healthy is supposed to look like, I just know I don't want to get bigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific weight or body shape that you're trying to maintain or achieve?\n\nPatient: Not an exact number, no. It's just... like I said before. If my work pants feel tight, or I catch my belly in the mirror, I just... I feel like I'm failing at everything. I just want to be that strong woman who used to handle transmissions and keep up with the guys. I don't want to lose that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: I... no. Not really. If I eat like that, I... I always get rid of it. It's been like that for a while now. For the last three months or so, there hasn't really been a week where I didn't do it at least once. Sometimes more, especially when my back is acting up and I'm stuck at home. But I always... I always make sure to throw it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to throw up after eating if you didn't care about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess... I haven't really thought about it like that. Um, for at least the past three months, I guess. There's hardly been a week where I didn't... you know, eat too much late at night and then... have to go get rid of it. Sometimes it's a few times a week. Especially lately, since I've been stuck at home more with a bad back. I kind of panic. Being laid up with my back, I just feel like I'm turning soft. So... I just start figuring out how to undo it. I used to be strong, you know? I could pull transmissions, keep up with the guys at the shop... and now I just see this person who can't even fit in her clothes right. It just... it takes over how I feel about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you knew for certain that your weight and body shape were perfectly healthy?\n\nPatient: I don't know... Yeah... I guess so. So I don't put on weight. After I eat too much, I just... go to the bathroom and make myself sick. It's just... I'm getting kind of scared that I can't stop doing it now.
